# P5 Stateful Workloads and Operations

Canonical boundary: P5 in `docs/community-roadmap.md`. Base protected main: `8c72c0b51c002d4e9c55fcc5898ecb461a796448`; P4 is accepted and remains unchanged. ODD, prospective RED/GREEN, existing ports/adapters/cipher/authority, RDD off. This tracker supplies work units, not a new approved phase denominator or time percentage.

## Roadmap outcomes and remaining acceptance

- Managed databases and catalog applications must use isolated, project-owned persistent storage.
- Generated workload credentials must use the existing encrypted secret repository and project scope; secret values must not enter metadata, audit, receipts or telemetry.
- Backup and retention must use an explicit policy, verified source/contents, durable inventory, scoped authority and protected restore references.
- Destructive restore must use existing confirmation and authority, verified immutable backup evidence, correlated audit and bounded recovery evidence.
- Health checks and telemetry must be workload-scoped, bounded and secret-safe.
- Actual integrated acceptance must verify the whole boundary before P5 can be marked accepted. Existing P3/P4 foundations alone grant no P5 completion credit.

## Approved database scope and reference behavior

Jerson explicitly selected parity with the five database engines documented by Dokploy: PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, MongoDB and Redis. This resolves the prior engine-selection question; no narrower PostgreSQL-only scope is assumed. The reference implementation is Dokploy commit `5be17d3dd01d9489521cde2eca7d997b14c54653`, inspected through public source and official documentation (Engram #25616).

Required operator flows follow Dokploy per engine: create/configure/deploy/update/stop/delete; generated connection credentials, internal connection details and explicitly configured external exposure; persistent volumes and environment configuration; scoped logs, resource monitoring and health; native engine-supported backup/restore with destination, schedule, manual test and retention count. Redis behavior must be checked against Dokploy's actual engine-specific implementation before claiming backup/restore parity; SQL dump behavior must not be inferred for it. The catalog remains a separate template/Compose capability, not a silently approved list of applications.

DeployLite retains its existing project authority, image policy, encrypted secret repository, cryptographic entropy, secret-safe telemetry, immutable backup evidence, restore-protection fences and destructive confirmation. Functional parity does not authorize copying weaker secret handling, bypassing required gates, opening ports, creating live credentials, or running local/production services. These approved flows remain implementation and acceptance requirements, not a claim that all five engines are already delivered.

R1 is metadata planning only and grants no deletion authority. The count policy is explicit and positive, with no guessed retention duration or default. Restore-protected archives are retained even outside the count. Archive IDs, SHA-256 content/manifest, timestamp, project, volume, destination, keep count and protected IDs bind the preview; inventory order is deterministic. Duplicate archive IDs, unknown/duplicate protection references, invalid count, foreign receipt scope and non-contract fields fail with a bounded generic error.

### S1 explicit native agent bootstrap — 2026-10-10

The agent now accepts trusted `DEPLOYLITE_MANAGED_DATABASE_RUNTIME` JSON with exactly `profiles` and `limits`. Absence disables native execution; malformed, incomplete or incompatible configuration fails before pool/server creation without echoing input. Configuration is operator-owned, never an HTTP command override. All five engine profiles are required and validated by the existing native factory/image policy. No default images or resource limits are supplied. The existing durable replay store, project-update authority validator, trust key and native authenticated routes remain the execution boundary. The capability is advertised only when the configured executor is attached. Startup itself performs no Docker operation.

`profiles` contains exactly `postgres`, `mysql`, `mariadb`, `mongodb` and `redis`, using the existing approved-profile schema: schemaVersion, profileId, matching engine, approved immutable image, supported dataPath/internal containerPort, credentialKeys and engine-specific backupStrategy. Existing agent image policy allows digest references on docker.io, ghcr.io and registry.example.com. CI profile pins remain fixture-only and are not production defaults. `limits` requires deadlineMs (1–60000), maxOutputBytes (1–1048576), maxContainers (1–128), pollIntervalMs (1–1000), cpus (0.1–2), memoryBytes (67108864–4294967296) and pidsLimit (16–1024); integer limits must be integers. No credentials, public port binding or unknown fields are accepted here.
